THE FOREIGN SERVICE

UNITED STATES OF AMERICA

for Japan
Tokyo, July 29, 1948
No. 477
UNCLASSIFIED
SUBJECT: Forwerding Publications Regrding Education in Japan.
894.42/7-2948
The Acting Political Adviser has the honor to forward as accom-
paniments to this despatch four ∞pies of Education in New Japan,
Volume I (text) and Volume II (appendix), publiahed by the CiviI
Information and Education Section of this Headquarters.
Part I, Volume I of this publication presents the historical
background of education in Japan, Part II presents a survey of the
pre-Occupation Japanese educational system, and Part III covers in
considerable detail the reconstruction of Japanese education during
the occupation.
Some of the more significant factors in the educationel reform
initiated during the occupation are:
(1) Removal of ultra-nationalistic and militaristic
elements and influences from the schools
(2)
Extension of free compulsory education from six
years to nine years
(3)
Authorization of co-education at all levels of
the school system
CSN
(4) Organizetion of vigorous teachers unions with a
major interest in the economic welfare of teachers.
(5) Revisions of curricula and courses of study at all
levele
(6)
Development of programs for adult education under
local leadership
(7)
Inauguration of daily broadcasts of radio programs
for elementary and seccndary schools and teachers
throughout the nation
(8)
Simplification of the written Japsnese language with
marked reduction in the number of senji characters in
common use and the introduction of i struction in the
Roman alphabet in the elementary schoos.
Volume II
UNCLASSIFIED

directives, Japanese laws, and documents implementing the program
of reform, together with statistical material on progress to date.
